4.2
Translations

The proof of cut elimination for the
\mathcal{G}L
calculi (where
L\in\left\{\mathsf{CS},\mathsf{CSM},\mathsf{ER}\right\}
) can be pictorically represented as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}L+\mathrm{Cut}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}^{\infty}L+\mathrm{Cut}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}^{\infty}L}}

In this subsection we will define the arrows on the top and bottom of the picture, i.e., how to translate from
\mathcal{G}L+\mathrm{Cut}
to
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}L+\mathrm{Cut}
and from
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}L
to
\mathcal{G}L
.
For the translation at the top we will use the admissibility of Löb’s rule in each of the wellfounded calculi, as shown in the next lemma.

{lemma}

We have that

1.

The rules

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{CS}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi

for
i\leq 1
are admissible in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
.

2.

The rules

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{CSM}}_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{CSM}}_{1}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

are admissible in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}+\mathrm{Cut}
.

3.

The rules

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{ER}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

for
i\leq 1
are admissible in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}+\mathrm{Cut}
.

Proof.

Proof of 1.
Let
\pi\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi\Rightarrow\phi
in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
.
The desired proof is

\pi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{CS}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi

\pi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi

Proof of 2.
For
\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{CSM}}_{0}
we construct the same proof as in
\mathsf{CS}
, let us show the admissibility of
\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{CSM}}_{1}
.
Assume we have the proof
\pi\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi\Rightarrow\phi
in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}+\mathrm{Cut}
.
The desired proof is

\pi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{CSM}}_{1}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi

\pi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

Proof of 3.
The proofs are completely analogous to the case of
\mathsf{CS}
.
∎

The translation is then defined via corecursion.

4.1
.

For any sequent
S
we have the following.

1.

If
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash S
, then
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash S
.

2.

If
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash S
, then
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CSM}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash S
.

3.

If
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash S
, then
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash S
.

Proof.

Let us write the proof for
\mathsf{CS}
, for
\mathsf{CSM}
and
\mathsf{ER}
the proof is completely analogous.
We define a function
\alpha
from proofs in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
to preproofs in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
by corecursion such that
\alpha(\pi)
has the same conclusion as
\pi
.
When the definition is finished we will justify that
\alpha(\pi)
is always a proof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
.
So let
\pi
be a proof in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
, we proceed by cases on the last rule
R
applied to
\pi
.

If
R
is one of
(\mathrm{ax})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{L})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{R})
,
({\to}\mathrm{L})
,
({\to}\mathrm{R})
,
(\mathrm{Cut})
then we define
\alpha
as follows:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 18.23798pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 0.50401pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\pi_{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$S_{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\cdots$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 0.50401pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\pi_{n-1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$S_{n-1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.9148pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to90.4342pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$R$}}\vskip-0.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 37.86295pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$S$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\quad\overset{\alpha}{\longmapsto}\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 19.8352pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\alpha(\pi_{0})$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 6.58339pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$S_{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\cdots$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\alpha(\pi_{n-1})$}\hskip 4.0pt}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 6.58339pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$S_{n-1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\vskip-0.9148pt\hbox{\hskip 6.58339pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to103.60098pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$R$}}\vskip-0.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 51.02972pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$S$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

since
R
is also a rule of
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
.
If
R
is
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{CS}}_{i})
then we define
\alpha
as follows

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 16.43741pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 5.72142pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 55.59703pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\pi_{0}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-2.71536pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to140.82332pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.77092pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{CS}}_{i}$}}\vskip-2.71536pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces\quad\overset{\alpha}{\longmapsto}\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 18.52965pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 21.87714pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 15.63164pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\alpha(\mathrm{L}\ddot{\mathrm{o}}\mathrm{b}^{\mathsf{CS}}_{i}(\pi_{0}))$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-2.71536pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to140.82332pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.77092pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}$}}\vskip-2.71536pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces

If
R
is one of
(\mathrm{ax})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{L})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{R})
,
({\to}\mathrm{L})
,
({\to}\mathrm{R})
,
(\mathrm{Cut})
then the height of the corecursive calls is strictly lower than the height of the input.
This is not the case if
R
is
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{CS}}_{i})
, but then there is an application of
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}
from the root to the corecursive call.
This imply that any infinite branch in
\alpha(\pi)
will have infinitely many applications of
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}
, so it will be a proof as desired.
∎

For the translation at the bottom of the picture, we will exploit the absence of
(\mathrm{Cut})
and we will use that the subformula property holds in the cut-free calculi.

{definition}

Let
\phi
be a formula, we define the set of its subformulas as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}(p)=\left\{p\right\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}(\bot)=\left\{\bot\right\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}(\phi_{0}\to\phi_{1})=\left\{\phi_{0}\to\phi_{1}\right\}\cup\mathrm{Sub}(\phi_{0})\cup\mathrm{Sub}(\phi_{1}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Sub}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi_{0})=\left\{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi_{0}\right\}\cup\mathrm{Sub}(\phi_{0}).

For any multiset
\Gamma
we will write
\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma)
to mean the set
\bigcup_{\phi\in\Gamma}\mathrm{Sub}(\phi)
and for a (possibly
\mathsf{ER}
-)sequent
\Gamma\gg\Delta
we will write
\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma\gg\Delta)
to mean
\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma)\cup\mathrm{Sub}(\Delta)
.

The following lemma follows by inspection of the shape of the rules.

{lemma}

[Local subformula property]
Let
R
be a rule of
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CSM}
,
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}
, or
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}
and
(S_{0},\ldots,S_{n-1},S)\in R
.
Then
\mathrm{Sub}(S_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Sub}(S)
for all
i<n
.

4.2
.

For any finite sets
\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{1}
, we have that

1.

\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}\vdash\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
implies
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
;

2.

\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CSM}\vdash\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
implies
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
;

3.

\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}\vdash\Gamma\gg\Delta
implies
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma\gg\Delta
.

Proof.

We prove the statement for
\mathsf{ER}
, the reasoning for
\mathsf{CS}
and
\mathsf{CSM}
is analogous.
Let
\pi\vdash\Gamma\gg\Delta
in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}
, we proceed by induction on the ordinal measure
\omega(\sum_{i\leq 1}|\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma\gg\Delta)\setminus\Lambda_{i}|)+\mathrm{lhg}(\pi)
and a case distinction on the last rule
R
applied in
\pi
.
If
R
is one of
(\mathrm{ax})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{L})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{R})
,
({\to}\mathrm{L})
,
({\to}\mathrm{R})
or
(\mathrm{ER}_{1,0})
, then it suffices to apply the induction hypothesis to the immediate subproofs and then apply
R
.
In more detail,
\pi
has the form

\pi_{0}

\Gamma_{0}\gg\Delta_{0}

\cdots

\pi_{n-1}

\Gamma_{n-1}\gg\Delta_{n-1}

R

\Gamma\gg\Delta

where
\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma_{i}\gg\Delta_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma\gg\Delta)
and
\mathrm{lhg}(\pi_{i})<\mathrm{lhg}(\pi)
.
Then by the induction hypothesis there are proofs
\tau_{i}\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma_{i}\gg\Delta_{i}
for
i<n
.
The desired proof is

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma_{0}\gg\Delta_{0}

\cdots

\tau_{n-1}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma_{n-1}\gg\Delta_{n-1}

R

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\Gamma\gg\Delta

If
R
is
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{0})
, then
\pi
is of the shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow_{0}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi,\Delta

where we denote the conclusion of
\pi_{0}
by
S_{0}
and the conclusion of
\pi
by
S
.
There are two cases, if
\phi\in\Lambda_{0}
then the desired proof is

\mathrm{Ax}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi,\Delta

If
\phi\not\in\Lambda_{0}
, then we have that
|\mathrm{Sub}(S_{0})\setminus(\Lambda_{0}\cup\left\{\phi\right\})|<|\mathrm{Sub}(S_{0})\setminus\Lambda_{0}|\leq|\mathrm{Sub}(S)\setminus\Lambda_{0}|
, where the second inequality holds because of
\mathrm{Sub}(S_{0})\subseteq\mathrm{Sub}(S)
.
For the same reason, we have
|\mathrm{Sub}(S_{1})\setminus\Lambda_{1}|\leq|\mathrm{Sub}(S)\setminus\Lambda_{1}|
. Hence, we can apply the induction hypothesis with sets
\Lambda_{0}\cup\left\{\phi\right\},\Lambda_{1}
to
\pi_{0}
.
Thus, we obtain a proof

\tau_{0}\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi\Rightarrow_{0}\phi
in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}
.
The desired proof is

\mathrm{Wk}(\tau_{0})

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi\Rightarrow_{0}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{ER}}_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Lambda_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Lambda_{1},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi,\Delta

The case when
R\text{ is }(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{1})
is analogous to the previous case.
∎

4.3
Local admissibility of cut

In this subsection, we prove cut elimination for the systems
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CSM}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}
.
Using the translations defined in Subsection
4.2
, we obtain cut elimination for
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}
and
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}
as a corollary.

4.3
.

\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
As a corollary
\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}
.

Proof.

We will show that
\mathrm{Cut}
is locally admissible in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
Let
\pi\vdash\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,\chi
and
\tau\vdash\chi,\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
We proceed by induction on the ordinal measure
\omega|\chi|+(\mathrm{lhg}(\pi)+\mathrm{lhg}(\tau))
and case analysis.
Most cases can be found in Appendix
B
, here we only show the cases that are unique for this calculus.

Assume
\pi
and
\tau
are of the following shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

where
\chi=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0}
,
\Gamma=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Delta=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}
and we assume without loss of generality that the auxiliary formulas of the last rule of
\pi
and
\tau
are equal, as otherwise we can use weakening on
\pi_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
to make them equal by making the rule instances maximal
4
4

4

A rule instance of a modal rule is called
maximal
if none of the weakening fomrulas is a
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits
-formula.
.
Note that this step is permited since it does not alter the local height and mantains the local cut-freeness of
\pi
and
\tau
.
The desired locally cut-free proof is

\mathrm{Wk}(\pi_{0})

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi,\chi_{0}

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

Assume

and
\tau
are of the following shape

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{\overline{i}}

,

∎

4.4
.

\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CSM}
.
As a corollary
\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}
.

Proof.

We will show that
\mathrm{Cut}
is locally admissible in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
Let
\pi\vdash\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,\chi
and
\tau\vdash\chi,\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
We proceed by induction on the ordinal measure
\omega|\chi|+(\mathrm{lhg}(\pi)+\mathrm{lhg}(\tau))
and by a case analysis.
Most cases can be found in Appendix
B
, here we only show the cases that are unique for this calculus.

Assume
\pi
and
\tau
are of the following shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CSM}}_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi,

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CSM}}_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

where
\chi=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0}
,
\Gamma=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Delta=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}
and we assume without loss of generality that the auxiliary formulas of the last rule of
\pi
and
\tau
are equal, as otherwise we can use weakening on

and

to make them equal by making the rule instances maximal.

and

.

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CSM}}_{1}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

where
\Gamma=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Delta=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}
and we assumed (again) without loss of generality that the auxiliary formulas of the last rule of
\pi
and
\tau
are equal, as otherwise we can use weakening on
\pi_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
to make them equal by making the rule instances maximal.
The desired locally cut-free proof is

\mathrm{Wk}(\pi_{0})

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi,\chi_{0}

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CSM}}_{1}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\chi_{0}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CSM}}_{1}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

∎

4.5
.

\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}
.
As a corollary
\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}
.

Proof.

We will show that
\mathrm{Cut}
is locally admissible in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
Let
\pi\vdash\Gamma\gg\Delta,\chi
and
\tau\vdash\chi,\Gamma\gg\Delta
in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.
We proceed by induction on the ordinal measure
\omega|\chi|+(\mathrm{lhg}(\pi)+\mathrm{lhg}(\tau))
and a case analysis.
Again, most cases can be found in Appendix
B
, here we only show the cases that are unique for this calculus.

Assume
\pi
and
\tau
are of the following shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow_{0}\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\Delta

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta

\mathrm{ER}_{1,0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta

where
\chi=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0}
,
\Gamma=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}
.
The desired locally cut-free proof is

{\Rightarrow_{1}}(\mathrm{Wk}(\pi_{0}))

\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta,\chi_{0}

\mathrm{ER}_{1,0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta,\chi_{0}

\pi_{0}

\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Rightarrow_{0}\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{0}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta

\mathrm{Cut}
(I.H.)

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta

\mathrm{Cut}
(I.H.)

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i},\Gamma^{\prime}\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta

where
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i\leq 1}\Sigma_{i}=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}
and the first use of the induction hypothesis is thanks to reducing the sum of local heights while the second use of the induction hypothesis is thanks to lowering the complexity of the cut formula.

Assume
\pi
and
\tau
are of the following shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

where
\chi=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0}
,
\Gamma=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Delta=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}
and we assumed without loss of generality that the auxiliary formulas of the last rule of
\pi
and
\tau
are equal, as otherwise we can use weakening on
\pi_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
to make them equal by making the rule instances maximal.
The desired locally cut-free proof is

\mathrm{Wk}(\pi_{0})

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi,\chi_{0}

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

Assume
\pi
and
\tau
are of the following shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{\overline{i}}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{\overline{i}}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

where
\chi=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0}
,
\Gamma=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}
,
\Delta=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}
and we assume again without loss of generality that the auxiliary formulas of the last rule of
\pi
and
\tau
are equal.
The desired locally cut-free proof is

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{i}\chi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{\overline{i}}\phi,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0}

\tau_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\chi_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{\overline{i}}\phi

\mathrm{Cut}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow_{\overline{i}}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{\overline{i}}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma_{\overline{i}},\Gamma^{\prime}\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\phi,\Delta^{\prime}

∎

The cut elimination for
\mathsf{ER}
allows us to improve the result of Theorem
3.2
to all sequents.

4.6
.

For any
\mathsf{ER}
-sequent
S
, we have that
\mathsf{ER}\vdash S^{\flat}
iff
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}\vdash S
.

Proof.

The if direction is proven by a simple induction in the height of the proof in
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}
using Lemma
3.2
, so let us show the only if direction.
If
S
is a
\Rightarrow_{0}
-sequent, then the result follows from Theorem
3.2
. Hence assume
S=\Gamma\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta
.
From
\mathsf{ER}\vdash\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}(\bigwedge\Gamma\to\bigvee\Delta)
, we obtain by Lemma
3.2
that
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}+\mathrm{Cut}\vdash{\Rightarrow_{0}\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}(\bigwedge\Gamma\to\bigvee\Delta)}
.
Then by one of the translations of Subsection
4.2
and cut elimination in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}
, we obtain that
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}\vdash{\Rightarrow_{0}\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}(\bigwedge\Gamma\to\bigvee\Delta)}
.
By the shape of the rules of
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}
(in particular the proof of the previous sequent must end in an application of
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}
), this implies that
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}\vdash{\Rightarrow_{1}\bigwedge\Gamma\to\bigvee\Delta}
and hence, by invertibility,
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}\vdash{\Gamma\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta}
.
Again, by one of the translations of Subsection
4.2
we can conclude
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}\vdash{\Gamma\Rightarrow_{1}\Delta}
.
∎

We finalize the section with a corollary which sums up all the results we have until now.

{corollary}

Let
S
be a sequent (when working with
\mathsf{ER}
an
\mathsf{ER}
-sequent).
We have that

1.

\mathsf{CS}\vdash S^{\flat}
iff
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}\;(+\mathrm{Cut})\vdash S
iff
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}\;(+\mathrm{Cut})\vdash S
;

2.

\mathsf{CSM}\vdash S^{\flat}
iff
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}\;(+\mathrm{Cut})\vdash S
iff
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CSM}\;(+\mathrm{Cut})\vdash S
;

3.

\mathsf{ER}\vdash S^{\flat}
iff
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}\;(+\mathrm{Cut})\vdash S
iff
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{ER}\;(+\mathrm{Cut})\vdash S
.

5
Interpolation

Having at our disposal the non-wellfounded calculi and the cut elimination theorem, we are prepared to show uniform Lyndon interpolation for
\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathsf{CSM}
and
\mathsf{ER}
.

For the uniformity, we will use a modification of proof search which we call
interpolation templates
.
The idea of an interpolation template as follows:
perform a proof search of the sequent at its root, but take into account that parts of the sequent may be missing.
For example, when evaluating which modal rules can be applied to a sequent
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta
in
\mathsf{CS}
, we have to take into account that the principal formula may be
outside

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta
.
This will provoke that in addition to the usual possible premise, we need to add two extra ones:
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow
in case the principal formula is outside and it is a
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}
-formula, and
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1}\Rightarrow
in case the principal formula is outside and it is a
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}
-formula.

To get not only the uniform property but also the Lyndon property for the interpolants, the use of the non-wellfounded calculi is essential.
This is because the diagonal formula in the wellfounded calculi ruins the polarity of the principal formula in the modal rules of
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{CSM}
and
\mathcal{G}\mathsf{ER}
.
The non-wellfounded calculi, not having a diagonal formula, behave well with respect to the polarities.

Finally, let us comment that the proof of uniform Lyndon interpolation for
\mathsf{CSM}
is really simple: we will use that
\mathsf{CSM}
can be interpreted in
\mathsf{CS}
and that
\mathsf{CS}
has uniform Lyndon interpolation.
It is an open question whether a similar interpretation of
\mathsf{ER}
in
\mathsf{CSM}
or
\mathsf{CS}
is possible.

5.1
Uniform Lyndon Interpolation for
\mathsf{CS}
and
\mathsf{CSM}

We start by defining the notion of interpolation template for
\mathsf{CS}
.
Notice that, even if it is based on an non-wellfounded system, we need the interpolation template to be a finite object, i.e., a cyclic proof and not any non-wellfounded proof.
Otherwise, the construction of the interpolant
would be hard if not impossible.

\mathrm{Emp}

\Rightarrow

[\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{s}_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{s}_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow]_{i\leq 1}

[\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{s}_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{s}_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\phi]_{i\leq 1,\phi\in\Theta_{i}}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta

where
\Gamma,\Delta\subseteq\mathrm{Var}
and
\Gamma\cap\Delta=\varnothing
.

Figure 9:
Interpolation template rules for
\mathsf{CS}

{definition}

A
\mathsf{CS}
-interpolation template
for
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
is a cyclic proof of
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
in the local progress sequent calculus with the propositional rules of Figure
2
and the rules of Figure
9
where progress is only made at the premises of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
.

We can show that any sequent has an interpolation template.
For that purpose let
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
be a sequent, define
|\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta|=\sum_{\phi\in\Gamma,\Delta}|\phi|
.
We will use this measure in the following lemma.

{lemma}

Every sequent has a
\mathsf{CS}
-interpolation template.

Proof.

Notice that if
R
is a rule of a
\mathsf{CS}
-interpolation templates and
(S_{0},\ldots,S_{n-1},S)
is an instance of
R
then
\mathrm{Sub}(S_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Sub}(S)
for
i<n
, so any preproof generated with rules of
\mathsf{CS}
-interpolation templates contains only formulas which are subformulas of the sequent at the root.
In addition, if
R
is distinct from
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
then
|S_{i}|<|S|
for
i<n
.

Also, notice that for any sequent
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
, there is always one rule of interpolation templates that can be applied to it.
Then we proceed as follows to construct an interpolation template for
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
:

1.

Stage 0. Start by putting
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
at the root of the tree without a rule.

2.

Stage
n+1
. For each leaf
w
at stage
n
without a rule do one of the following (in order):

(a)

If there is a
v<w
with the same sequent, make
w
a repeat node pointing to
v
.

(b)

Otherwise, select a rule
R
and a rule instance
(S_{0},\ldots,S_{n-1},S)\in R
such that
w
is annotated with sequent
S
. Annotate
w
with the rule
R
and create new nodes
w0
, …,
w(n-1)
immediate successors of
w
annotated with sequent
S_{0},\ldots,S_{n-1}
respectively.

First, we show that this process finishes.

Suppose otherwise, then at the limit we would have an non-wellfounded tree with at least one infinite branch
(w_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Let
w_{i}
be annotated with sequent
S_{i}
and rule
R_{i}
, notice that all the
S_{i}
s are pairwise different, as otherwise the infinite branch would have been closed during the construction with a repeat.
If there are only finitely many
i
s such that
R_{i}=(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
then there is a maximum
i_{0}
such that for any
j>i_{0}
we have that
R_{i}\neq(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
.
Then
|S_{i_{0}}|>|S_{i_{0}+1}|>\cdots>|S_{i_{0}+k}|>\cdots
, which is impossible.
So there are infinitely many
i
s such that
R_{i}=(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
, let
\left\{i_{j}\right\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be those
i
s.
Let
m
be the cardinality of
\mathrm{Sub}(\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta)
, thanks to the subformula property and since premises of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
are determined by two sets of formulas (
\Sigma^{s}_{0}
and
\Sigma^{s}_{1}
) and a possible choice of formula (
\phi
), we have that the possible number of premises of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
in
T
is
4^{m}(m+1)=2^{m}2^{m}(m+1)
.
However, in
(w_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
there are infinitely many premises of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
, so at least there is a repeated sequent, a contradiction.

The created tree
T
is clearly a cyclic preproof.
Given a node
w
of
T
let
S_{w}
be the sequent at
w
and
R_{w}
the rule at
w
.
Asumme
w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
such that for each
v\in(w^{\circ},w]
is non-progressing.
Then for each
v\in[w^{\circ},w)
,
R_{v}\neq(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
so
|S_{w^{\circ}}|>|S_{w}|=|S_{w^{\circ}}|
, a contradiction.
∎

We fix vocabularies
V_{+}
and
V_{-}
for which we want to calculate the uniform Lyndon interpolant.
Once we have the interpolation template we will divide the construction of the interpolant in two parts.
First, we will calculate a formula at each node
w
of the interpolation template which we call
preinterpolant at
w
.
Then, using the preinterpolants, we will create a equational system which will be solvable in
\mathsf{CS}
.
The interpolant will then be obtained by applying the substitution which solves the equational system to the preinterpolant at the root.

We have no way of interpolating repeat nodes, so in principle we would get stuck in them.
However, due to the uniqueness of interpolants we know that the interpolant at a repeat node must be logically equivalent to the interpolant at its cyclic companion, in other words, we obtain an equation.
Solving all the equations that appear in the interpolation template will just be solving an equational system.

We start with the definitions of preinterpolants.

{definition}

Let
T
be an interpolation template and
w
a node of
T
, let us denote the sequent at
w
as
\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w}
.
We will annotate each of the sequents of
T
with a formula
\kappa
called the
preinterpolant at
\kappa
, denoted as
\kappa:\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w}
.
We proceed by induction on the tree structure of
T
.
If
w
is a repeat node of shape

\mathrm{Rep}

\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w}

then
\kappa_{w}
will be a fresh propositional variable
x_{w}
.
This variable shall not appear in any formula of
T
and for repeat nodes
w,v
such that
w\neq v
we must have that
x_{w}\neq x_{v}
.
In case
w
is not a repeat node, we proceed by cases on the rule at
w
. We give the construction as follows (the preinterpolant at the conclusion is defined recursively from the preinterpolants at the premises):

\mathrm{ax}

\bot:p,\Gamma\Rightarrow p,\Delta

\mathrm{Emp}

\top:{\Rightarrow}

{\bot}\mathrm{L}

\bot:p,\Gamma\Rightarrow p,\Delta

\kappa:\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta

{\bot}\mathrm{R}

\kappa:\Gamma\Rightarrow\bot,\Delta

\kappa_{0}:\Gamma\Rightarrow\phi,\Delta

\kappa_{1}:\psi,\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta

{\to}\mathrm{L}

\kappa_{0}\vee\kappa_{1}:\phi\to\psi,\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta

\kappa:\phi,\Gamma\Rightarrow\psi,\Delta

{\to}\mathrm{R}

\kappa:\Gamma\Rightarrow\phi\to\psi,\Delta

\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}:\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{s}_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{s}_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow

[\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi}:\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{s},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{s}_{i}\Rightarrow\phi]_{i\leq 1,\phi\in\Theta_{i}}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+}

\kappa:\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta

where

\kappa:=\bigwedge_{i\leq 1}\left(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}\wedge\bigwedge\limits_{\phi\in\Theta_{i}}\mathop{\text{\scalebox{1.1}{$\meddiamond$}}}\nolimits_{i}\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi}\right)\wedge\bigwedge(\Gamma\cap V_{+})\wedge\bigwedge\neg(\Delta\cap V_{-})

Let
T
be an interpolation template, we proceed to define the equational system attached to
T
.
For each node
w
let
\kappa_{w}
be the preinterpolant at
w
.
Remember that the
height of a node
w
, denoted
\mathrm{hg}(w)
, is the height of the subtree generated at
w
.
Then leafs have height
0
and the height of the root is the same as the height of the tree.
For each
i\in\mathbb{N}
let
\bar{x}_{i}
be an arbitrary enumeration of
\left\{x_{w}\mid w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T),\mathrm{hg}(w^{\circ})=i\right\}
.
If
\mathrm{hg}(T)=H
, then we define
\bar{x}_{T}=\bar{x}_{0}\bar{x}_{1}\cdots\bar{x}_{H}
,
\bar{x}_{T}
is an anumeration of
\left\{x_{w}\mid w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)\right\}
.
We define the
equational system of
T
, denoted
\mathcal{E}_{T}
, as

\left\{(x_{w},+,\kappa_{w})\mid w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)\right\}.

In the following lemma we show that it
\mathcal{E}_{T}
is solvable in
\mathsf{CS}
.

{lemma}

\mathcal{E}_{T}
is a positive modalized Lyndon equational system over
(\bar{x}_{T},V_{+},V_{-})
.
As a corollary,
\mathcal{E}_{T}
has a solution in
\mathsf{CS}
.

Proof.

For each node
w
of
T
let
\kappa_{w}
be the preinterpolant at
w
.
By induction on the height of
w
, we can show that
\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\kappa_{w})\subseteq V_{+}\cup\left\{x_{v}\mid v\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)\right\}
and
\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\kappa_{w})\subseteq V_{-}
, so it is clearly positive.

It remains to show that it is modalized. For this it suffices to prove that for every
w,v\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
if
\mathrm{hg}(v^{\circ})\leq\mathrm{hg}(w^{\circ})
, then
\kappa_{w}
is modalized in
x_{v}
.
We have the following facts which are easy to establish:

1.

for each
v\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
and node
w
if
x_{v}
occurs at
\kappa_{w}
then
w\leq v
,

2.

for each node
w
with rule
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
we have that
\kappa_{v}
is modalized in
\left\{x_{w}\mid w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)\right\}
,

3.

for each
v\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
and node
w\not\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
with immediate successors
w_{0},\ldots,w_{n-1}
, if
\kappa_{w_{0}}
, …,
\kappa_{w_{n-1}}
are modalized in
x_{v}
then
\kappa_{w}
is modalized in
v
.

If
\mathrm{hg}(v^{\circ})\leq\mathrm{hg}(w^{\circ})
we have that either
v^{\circ}\leq w^{\circ}
or
v^{\circ}
and
w^{\circ}
are incomparable.
Assume
v^{\circ}
and
w^{\circ}
are incomparable.
Then it must also be the case that
v
and
w^{\circ}
are incomparable, so
\kappa_{w^{\circ}}
is modalized in
x_{v}
as
x_{v}
does not occur in
\kappa_{w^{\circ}}
.
Finally, assume that
v^{\circ}\leq w^{\circ}
.
We know there must be a node
u\in[v^{\circ},v)
which is annotated with
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+}
so
\kappa_{u}
is modalized in
x_{v}
.
Then, using the third fact, we can show that (by induction) the preinterpolant of any node below
u
is modalized in
x_{v}
(since it is not a repeat node and all the preinterpolants of the immediate successors are also modalized in
x_{v}
, either by virtue of the node not being comparable with
v
or by the induction hypothesis).
We can conclude, as
w^{\circ}\leq v^{\circ}\leq u
, that
\kappa_{w^{\circ}}
is modalized in
x_{v}
.
∎

Finally, we have all the necessary tools to define the interpolant.

{definition}

[Interpolant]
Given an interpolation template
T
we define the interpolant of
T
, denoted
\iota_{T}
, as the formula obtained by applying the substitution solving
\mathcal{E}_{T}
to the preinterpolant at the root of
T
.

Strictly speaking the definition of the interpolant is not unique.
For an interpolation template
T
,
\iota_{T}
will depend on the choice of the solution of
T
.
However, any two interpolants will be
\mathsf{CS}
-logically equivalent, so this lack of unicity should not worry us.

With the interpolant defined there is only one task left, we need to verify that it truly fulfills the necessary interpolation properties.
Notice that
\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\iota_{T})\subset V_{+}
and
\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\iota_{T})
holds from having applied a solution of an equational system over
(\bar{x}_{T},V_{+},V_{-})
to a formula
\kappa
with
\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\kappa)\subseteq V_{+}\cup\left\{x_{w}\mid w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)\right\}
and
\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\kappa)\subseteq V_{-}
together with Lemma
2.2
.
The other two properties of interpolants will be proven thanks to the following two theorems.

5.1
.

Let
T
be an interpolation template for
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
.
Then
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}\vdash\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,\iota_{T}
.

Proof.

Given a node
w
of
T
let us denote the sequent at
w
as
\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w}
, the preinterpolant of
T
at
w
as
\kappa_{w}
and assume
(\cdot)^{*}
is a solution of
\mathcal{E}_{T}
.
We define a function
\alpha
such that if
w
is a node of
T
then
\alpha(w)
is a preproof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}+\mathrm{Wk}
of
\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w},\kappa^{*}_{w}
.
Then we will show that
\alpha(w)
is in fact a proof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}+\mathrm{Wk}
and then using that
\mathrm{Wk}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}
and that
\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
we obtain the desired proof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.

We proceed to define
\alpha
by corecursion and cases.
First, assume
w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
then
w^{\circ}
has the same sequent as
w
and
\kappa_{w}=x_{w}
.
Also, by virtue of
(\cdot)^{*}
being a solution of
\mathcal{E}_{T}
, we have that
\mathsf{CS}\vdash x^{*}_{w}\leftrightarrow\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}}
.
Then we define
\alpha
as

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 7.11299pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 23.94148pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-1.88701pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to55.88298pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.94257pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathrm{Rep}$}}\vskip-1.88701pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$x_{w}:\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces\quad\overset{\alpha}{\longmapsto}\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 30.86485pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.31654pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 10.30896pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\alpha(w^{\circ})$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,\kappa^{*}_{w}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to71.4513pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathrm{Wk}$}}\vskip-0.97035pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,x^{*}_{w},\kappa^{*}_{w}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 30.92877pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\kappa^{*}_{w},\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,x^{*}_{w},$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.9148pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to178.68974pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathrm{Cut}$}}\vskip-0.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 61.95892pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta,x^{*}_{w}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

where the proof
\tau
exists since
\mathsf{CS}\vdash x^{*}_{w}\leftrightarrow\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}}
.

Now assume
w
is not a repetition node and let
R
be the rule at
w
.
Below there are the definitions when
R
is
(\mathrm{ax})
,
(\mathrm{Emp})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{L})
,
({\bot}\mathrm{R})
,
({\to}\mathrm{L})
or
({\to}\mathrm{R})
.

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 22.22221pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 21.95825pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$w0$}\hskip Finally, let us define
\alpha(w)
when
R=(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
.
Then
w
is of shape

\left[\begin{matrix}w^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}\\
\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}:\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{s}_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{s}_{\overline{i}}\Rightarrow\end{matrix}\right]_{i\leq 1}

\left[\begin{matrix}w^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi}\\
\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi}:\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{s},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{s}_{i}\Rightarrow\phi\end{matrix}\right]_{i\leq 1,\phi\in\Theta_{i}}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+}

\kappa_{w}:\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta

where

\kappa^{*}_{w}:=\bigwedge_{i\leq 1}\left(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}})^{*}\wedge\bigwedge_{\phi\in\Theta_{i}}\mathop{\text{\scalebox{1.1}{$\meddiamond$}}}\nolimits_{i}(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi})^{*}\right)\wedge\bigwedge(\Gamma\cap V_{+})\wedge\bigwedge\neg(\Delta\cap V_{-}).

Since
\kappa^{*}_{w}
is a conjunction of formulas it suffices to construct a preproof for each conjunct and join them with
({\wedge}\mathrm{R})
.

Preproof for
p\in\Gamma\cap V_{+}
and for
\neg q
where
q\in\Delta\cap V_{-}
.
The desired preproof, respectively, are

\mathrm{ax}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma,\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta,p

\mathrm{ax}

q,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma,\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta

{\neg}\mathrm{R}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma,\Gamma\Rightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta,\neg q

Finally, let us argue that
\alpha(w)
is always a proof and not only a preproof.
Given a node
w
assign to it the measure
\omega|\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w}|+\ell(w)
where
\ell(w)
is the length of
w
as a sequence of natural numbers.
We notice that this measure always decreases from
\alpha(w)
to its corecursive call, except when
w
is annotated with the rule
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{+})
.
However, in this case we will find that progress is made (i.e., there is an application of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i})
) from the root of the preproof fragment given by
\alpha(w)
to the corecursive calls.
This implies that any infinite branch will have infinitely many applications of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4CS}}_{i})
, as otherwise we will infinitely decrease the measure on corecursive calls.
∎

5.2
.

Let
T
be an interpolation template for
\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta
and
\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda
be a sequent such that
\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda)\subseteq V_{b}
for
b\in\left\{+,-\right\}
.
Then
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}\vdash\Gamma,\Xi\Rightarrow\Delta,\Lambda
implies
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}\vdash\iota_{T},\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda
.

Proof.

Given a node
w
of
T
let us denote the sequent at
w
as
\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w},
, the preinterpolant at
w
as
\kappa_{w}
and assume
(\cdot)^{*}
is a solution of
\mathcal{E}_{T}
.
We define a function
\beta
such that if
w
is a node of
T
and
\pi
is a proof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
of
\Gamma_{w},\Xi\Rightarrow\Delta_{w},\Lambda
where
\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda
is a sequent with
\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda)\subseteq V_{b}
for
b\in\left\{+,-\right\}
, then
\beta(w,\pi)
is a preproof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}+\mathrm{Wk}
of
\kappa^{*}_{w},\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda
.
Then we will show that
\beta(w,\pi)
is in fact a proof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}+\mathrm{Cut}+\mathrm{Wk}
and then using that
\mathrm{Wk}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
and that
\mathrm{Cut}
is eliminable in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
we obtian the desired proof in
\mathcal{G}^{\infty}\mathsf{CS}
.

We proceed to define
\beta
by corecursion and cases.
First, assume
w\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
then
w^{\circ}
has the same sequent as
w
and
\kappa_{w}=x_{w}
.
Also, by virtue of
(\cdot)^{*}
being a solution of
\mathcal{E}_{T}
, we have that
\mathsf{CS}\vdash x^{*}_{w}\leftrightarrow\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}}
.
Then we define
\beta
as

\left(\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 7.11299pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 23.94148pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-1.88701pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to55.88298pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.94257pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathrm{Rep}$}}\vskip-1.88701pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$x_{w}:\Gamma\Rightarrow\Delta$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces,\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 7.15277pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 23.46925pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\pi$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma,\Xi\Rightarrow\Delta,\Lambda$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\ignorespaces\right)\quad\overset{\beta}{\longmapsto}\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 30.86485pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 31.14406pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\tau$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$x^{*}_{w},\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda,\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 8.31654pt\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 7.21117pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\beta(w^{\circ},\pi)$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}\hbox{\vbox{\vskip 1.0pt}}\lower 0.0pt\hbox{}}\vskip 2.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}},\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to74.65964pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.97035pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathrm{Wk}$}}\vskip-0.97035pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}},x^{*}_{w},\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-0.9148pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}\hbox to163.77304pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 2.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt$\mathrm{Cut}$}}\vskip-0.91478pt\hbox{\hskip 54.9867pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$x^{*}_{w},\Xi\Rightarrow\Lambda$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

where the proof
\tau
exists since
\mathsf{CS}\vdash x^{*}_{w}\leftrightarrow\kappa^{*}_{w^{\circ}}
.

Now assume that
w\not\in\mathrm{Rep}(T)
and let

Below there are the definitions when

is
(\mathrm{ax})
