\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma,\Xi\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta,\Lambda

where
\Sigma^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\Sigma_{i}
,
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\overline{i}}\subseteq\Sigma_{\overline{i}}
and
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\subseteq\Xi
.
Then the desired preproof is

\beta(w^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi},\pi_{0})

(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi})^{*},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\Rightarrow_{i}

{\neg}\mathrm{R}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\Rightarrow_{i}\neg(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi})^{*}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}

\Xi\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\neg(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi})^{*},\Lambda

{\neg}\mathrm{L}

\mathop{\text{\scalebox{1.1}{$\meddiamond$}}}\nolimits_{i}(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}}_{\phi})^{*},\Xi\gg\Lambda

\mathrm{Wk}+{\wedge}\mathrm{L}

\kappa^{*}_{w},\Xi\gg\Lambda

•

Last rule of
\pi
is
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i})
and the principal formula is in
\Lambda
.
Then
\pi
has the following shape

\pi_{0}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Sigma^{\prime}_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Sigma^{\prime}_{\overline{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Sigma_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Sigma_{1},\Gamma,\Xi\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\Theta_{0},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}\Theta_{1},\Delta,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Lambda^{\prime}

where
\Lambda=\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Lambda^{\prime}
,
\Sigma^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq\Sigma_{i}
,
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\overline{i}}\subseteq\Sigma_{\overline{i}}
and
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\subseteq\Xi
.
Then the desired preproof is

\beta(w^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\pi_{0})

(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}})^{*},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathrm{Wk}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}})^{*},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\Xi_{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{\overline{i}}}\Rightarrow_{i}\phi

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}

\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}(\kappa^{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.3014pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}})^{*},\Xi\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Lambda^{\prime}

\mathrm{Wk}+{\wedge}\mathrm{L}

\kappa^{*}_{w},\Xi\gg\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{i}\phi,\Lambda^{\prime}

Finally, let us argue that
\beta(w,\pi)
is always a proof and not only a preproof.
Given a node
w
assign to it the measure
\omega^{2}|\Gamma_{w}\Rightarrow\Delta_{w}|_{\mathrm{Sat}}+\omega\ell(w)+\mathrm{lhg}(\pi)
where
\ell(w)
is the length of
w
as a sequence of natural numbers.
We notice that this measure always decreases from
\beta(w,\pi)
to its corecursive calls, except when
w
is annotated with the rule
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{+})
and the last rule of
\pi
is
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i}
.
However, in this case we will find that progress is made (i.e., there is an application of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i})
) from the root of the preproof fragment given by
\beta(w,\pi)
to the corecursive calls.
This implies that any infinite branch will have infinitely many applications of
(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits^{\mathsf{K4ER}}_{i})
, as otherwise we will infinitely decrease the measure on corecursive calls.
∎

Then following theorem follows the same proof as Theorem
5.3
using Theorems
5.5
and
5.6
instead of Theorems
5.1
and
5.2
.

5.7
.

\mathsf{ER}
has uniform Lyndon interpolation.

6
Conclusion and Future Work

We provided simple cut-free sequent calculi for the bimodal provability logics
\mathsf{CS}
,
\mathsf{CSM}
and
\mathsf{ER}
.
An interesting technical contribution is that we only had to consider multiple kinds of sequents to deal with
\mathsf{ER}
, but no other mechanism was needed.
Moreover, we introduced non-wellfounded calcluli for these logics to establish cut-elimination and uniform Lyndon interpolation for all three logics.
These are the first results of this kind for bimodal provability logics with ’usual’ provability predicates.

For future work, we would like to continue exploring the realm of bimodal provabiltiy logics by proof-theoretic means.
Designing nice sequent calculi for these logics could also provide alternative proofs for facts that are usually established via Kripke models.
This can be of particular interest for logics like
\mathsf{ER}
that do not have a (non-general) Kripke semantics.
Additionally, we leave open the question of finding an interpretation of
\mathsf{ER}
in
\mathsf{CSM}
that preserves polarity of variables.
We note that the interpretation

\phi\mapsto\left(\bigwedge\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquaredot$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\left\{\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{1}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\psi\to\psi)\mid\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{0}\psi\in\mathrm{Sub}(\phi)\right\}\to\phi\right),

which can be proven to be correct using the subformula property of our sequent calculi (or through the semantics), does not preserve the polarity of variables.

We would like to thank Justus Becker for our discussions on the proof theory of provability logic.
In addition, the questions he raised in
[
5
]
served as a great inspiration and motivation for this paper.
Also, we would like to thank Anupam Das for calling our attention to Bekić Theorem (see footnote at page
2
).

Borja Sierra Miranda and Thomas Studer are supported by the Swiss National Science Foundation project 200021_214820
Non-wellfounded and cyclic proof theory
.

Appendix A
Solving Equational Systems

In Subsection
2.2
we claimed that it was enough to show that a logic
L
has basic Lyndon fixpoints to obtain that
L
has positive modalized Lyndon fixpoints and every positive modalized Lyndon equational system has a solution in
L
.
Here we give the details of these results.

We start with a lemma.

{lemma}

Let
L
be a bimodal normal logic with basic Lyndon fixpoints and closed under substitutions.
Every basic Lyndon equational system has a solution in
L
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}=\left\{(p_{i},b_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\phi_{i})\mid i<n\right\}
be a basic Lyndon equational system over
(\bar{p},V_{+},V_{-})
, remember that
B_{+}=\left\{p_{i}\mid i<n,b_{i}={+}\right\}
,
B_{-}=\left\{p_{i}\mid i<n,b_{i}={-}\right\}
.
We proceed by induction on
n
, the number of unknowns.

Take
\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\phi_{0}(p_{0},\ldots,p_{n-1})
, we know that it has a Lyndon fixpoint
\psi_{0}
with respect to
p_{0}
in
L
.
Note that if
b_{0}=b_{i}
then
p_{0}\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi_{i})
and if
b_{0}\neq b_{i}
then
p_{0}\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\phi_{i})
.
Then, using Lemma
2.2
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\phi_{i})\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\}\cup\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\psi_{0})=\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\phi_{i})\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\}\cup\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\psi_{0})\subseteq V_{+}\cup B_{+}\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\},

\displaystyle\hskip 398.33858pt\text{if }b_{0}=b_{i},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\phi_{i})\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\}\cup\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b_{i}}}(\psi_{0})=\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\phi_{i})\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\}\cup\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\psi_{0})\subseteq V_{+}\cup B_{+}\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\},

\displaystyle\hskip 398.33858pt\text{if }b_{0}\neq b_{i}.

We have a similar argument to show that
\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b_{i}}}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])\subseteq V_{-}\cup B_{-}\setminus\left\{p_{0}\right\}
.
So we have that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\left\{(p_{i},b_{i},\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])\mid 1\leq i<n\right\}
is a basic Lyndon equational system over
(p_{1}\cdots p_{n-1},V_{+},V_{-})
and it is solvable in
L
by the induction hypothesis, let
(\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n})
be a solution in
L
of it.
Let us define
\chi_{0}=\psi_{0}[\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}]
, then we claim that
(\chi_{0},\ldots,\chi_{n})
is a solution of
\mathcal{E}
.

First, note that we already have that
\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\chi_{i})\subseteq V_{+}
and
\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b_{i}}}(\chi_{i})\subseteq V_{-}
for
1\leq i<n
.
We notice that for
1\leq i<n
we have that if
b_{i}=b_{0}
then
p_{i}\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\psi_{0})
and if
b_{i}\neq b_{0}
then
p_{i}\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\psi_{0})
(it suffices to do cases on
b_{i}
and
b_{0}
).
Using Lemma
2.2
we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\chi_{0})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\psi_{0})\setminus\left\{p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}\right\}\cup\bigcup_{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}1\leq i<n\\
b_{i}=b_{0}\end{matrix}}\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\chi_{i})\cup\bigcup_{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}1\leq i<n\\
b_{i}\neq b_{0}\end{matrix}}\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b_{0}}}(\chi_{i})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\phi_{0})\setminus\left\{p_{0},\ldots,p_{n}\right\}\cup\bigcup_{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}1\leq i<n\\
b_{i}=b_{0}\end{matrix}}\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\chi_{i})\cup\bigcup_{\footnotesize\begin{matrix}1\leq i<n\\
b_{i}\neq b_{0}\end{matrix}}\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{i}}(\chi_{i})\subseteq V_{+}.

where we used that
\mathrm{Voc}_{b_{0}}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\phi_{0})\subseteq V_{+}\cup B_{+}
.
We have an anologous reasoning for showing that
\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b_{0}}}(\chi_{0})\subseteq V_{-}
, so the desired polarity conditions hold.

Finally, we show the desired equivalences.
We have
L\vdash\chi_{i}\leftrightarrow(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])[\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}]
for
1\leq i<n
.
Using that
p_{0}\neq p_{i}
for
1\leq i<n
we obtain that

\displaystyle(\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])[\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}]

\displaystyle=\phi_{i}[\psi_{0}[\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}]/p_{0},\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}]

\displaystyle=\phi_{i}[\chi_{0}/p_{0},\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}],

as desired.
All left to show that
L\vdash\chi_{0}\leftrightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\phi_{0}[\chi_{0}/p_{0},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}]
.
We have that, as
\psi_{0}
is a fixpoint,
L\vdash\psi_{0}\leftrightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\phi_{0}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}]
.
As
L
is closed under substitutions we obtain that

L\vdash\chi_{0}\leftrightarrow(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\phi_{0}[\psi_{0}/p_{0}])[\chi_{1}/p_{1},\ldots,\chi_{n}/p_{n}],

and we can use the same equalities as before since
p_{0}\neq p_{i}
for
1\leq i<n
.
∎

A.1
.

Let
L
be a bimodal normal logic with basic Lyndon fixpoints and closed under substitutions.
We have that

1.

L
has positive modalized Lyndon fixpoins.

2.

Positive modalized Lyndon equational systems have solution in
L
.

Proof.

Proof of 1.

5
5

5

This proof follows the proof in
[
28
]
for
\mathsf{GL}
, with the addition of the condition on the polarity of variables.

Let
\phi(p)
be a formula that is modalized in
p
and positive in
p
.
Then
\phi(p)
can be written as
\phi^{\prime}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\psi_{0}(p),\ldots,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{n-1}}\psi_{n-1}(p),\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{k_{0}}\chi_{0}(p),\ldots,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{k_{m-1}}\chi_{m-1}(p))
,
where the variables in
\bar{q}\bar{r}
are pairwise distinct,
\phi^{\prime}
does not contain any modality, and
\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\phi^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\phi)\setminus\left\{p\right\}\cup\bar{q}
,
\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi^{\prime})\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi)\setminus\left\{p\right\}\cup\bar{r}
(so
p
does not occur in
\phi^{\prime}
).
In particular this means that
\bar{q}\cap\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi^{\prime})=\varnothing
and
\bar{r}\cap\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\phi^{\prime})=\varnothing
.
By Lemma
2.2
we have that for
b\in\left\{+,-\right\}

\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\phi)=\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\phi^{\prime})\setminus\bar{q}\bar{r}\cup\bigcup_{i<n}\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\psi_{i})\cup\bigcup_{i<m}\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b}}(\chi_{i})\quad\text{so}\quad\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\psi_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\phi)\text{ and }\mathrm{Voc}_{b}(\chi_{i})\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{\overline{b}}(\phi).

As
p\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi)
we obtain straightforwardly that
p\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\psi_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad p\not\in\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\chi_{i})
.

Consider the following equational system

\left\{(q_{i},+,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\psi_{i}(\phi^{\prime}))\mid i<n\right\}\cup\left\{(r_{i},-,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits\chi_{k_{i}}(\phi^{\prime}))\mid i<m\right\}.

Using Lemma
2.2
with the previous observations, it is straightforward to check that is a simple Lyndon
(\bar{q}\bar{r},\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\phi)\setminus\left\{p\right\},\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi)\setminus\left\{p\right\})
-equational system.
Thus it has a solution
(\cdot)^{*}
in
L
.
Let us define
\eta=\phi^{\prime}(q^{*}_{0},\ldots,q^{*}_{n-1},r^{*}_{0},\ldots,r^{*}_{m-1})
, by Lemma
2.2
we know that
\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\eta)\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{+}(\phi)\setminus\left\{p\right\}
and
\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\eta)\subseteq\mathrm{Voc}_{-}(\phi)\setminus\left\{p\right\}
.
Also, since
(\cdot)^{*}
is a solution of the equational system, we have that
L\vdash q^{*}_{i}\leftrightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{i}}\psi_{i}(\eta)
for
i<n
and
L\vdash r^{*}_{i}\leftrightarrow\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{k_{i}}\chi_{i}(\eta)
for
i<m
. So we obtain (using propositional reasoning) that

L\vdash\eta\leftrightarrow\phi^{\prime}(\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{0}}\psi_{0}(\eta),\ldots,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{j_{n-1}}\psi_{n-1}(\eta),\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{k_{0}}\chi_{0}(\eta),\ldots,\mathop{\text{\raisebox{-0.43057pt}{\scalebox{1.3}{$\medsquare$}}}}\nolimits_{k_{m-1}}\chi_{m-1}(\eta)).

In other words,
L\vdash\eta\leftrightarrow\phi(\eta)
, so
\eta
is a Lyndon fixpoint of
\phi
with respect to
p
.

Proof of 2. The proof is similar to the second point of Lemma
A
using that if
\phi_{i}
is modalised in
p_{0},\ldots,p_{i}
then
\phi_{i}[\psi/p_{0}]
is also modalised in
p_{0},\ldots,p_{i}
.
∎
